## GHSA-p34x-fmph-9fjx · flyto-core

2026-11-20 · Python · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The application allowed user-controlled paths for file write operations without sufficient validation. An incomplete fix for a previous vulnerability (CVE-2026-67429) still permitted absolute paths or paths that bypassed simple '..' checks, leading to arbitrary file writes outside the intended sandbox directory.

### Impact

An attacker could write arbitrary files to any location on the file system accessible by the application, potentially leading to remote code execution, denial of service, or data corruption.

### Key diff

```
-    output_path = params.get('output_path', '/tmp/output.csv')
+    output_path = params.get('output_path', '/tmp/output.csv')
+    output_path = validate_path_with_env_config(output_path)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `validate_path_with_env_config` utility function. This function is applied to all user-controlled file paths before any file operations (read or write) to ensure they are confined within the `FLYTO_SANDBOX_DIR` environment variable, preventing path traversal.

## GHSA-mc8w-wjhw-45x5 · ep_etherpad-lite

2026-11-20 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an unconditional replacement of backslashes with forward slashes in file paths. While `sanitizePathname()` removed '..\' sequences on Windows, on POSIX systems, '..\' is treated as a literal filename component. The subsequent `replace(/\\/g, '/')` operation on POSIX systems transformed these literal '..\' components into '..//' or '../' path traversals *after* the initial sanitization, allowing an attacker to read arbitrary files outside the intended directory.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could craft a malicious request to read any file on the server's filesystem, potentially leading to sensitive data exposure or further system compromise.

### Key diff

```
-      filename = filename.replace(/\\/g, '/');
+      if (path.sep === '\\') filename = filename.replace(/\\/g, '/');
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a conditional check to ensure that the backslash-to-forward-slash conversion (`filename = filename.replace(/\\/g, '/')`) only occurs on Windows systems (`if (path.sep === '\\')`). This prevents the unintended reintroduction of path traversal sequences on POSIX systems where backslashes are treated as ordinary filename characters.

## GHSA-wj7x-58c2-5793 · github.com/daptin/daptin

2026-11-20 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.6 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The YJS collaboration endpoints, particularly `/yjs/:documentName` and `/live/:typename/:referenceId/content/yjs`, lacked proper authorization checks. An authenticated user could access or modify any YJS document by knowing its name or reference ID, regardless of whether they had permissions to the underlying resource.

### Impact

An attacker could gain unauthorized read or write access to sensitive data stored in YJS documents, potentially leading to data leakage, modification, or corruption across different resources.

### Key diff

```
--- a/server/endpoint_yjs.go
+++ b/server/endpoint_yjs.go
@@ -27,13 +101,29 @@ func InitializeYjsResources(store ydb.Store, defaultRouter *gin.Engine,
 	defaultRouter.GET("/yjs/:documentName", func(ginContext *gin.Context) {
 
 		sessionUser := ginContext.Request.Context().Value("user")
-		if sessionUser == nil {
+		user, ok := sessionUser.(*auth.SessionUser)
+		if !ok || user == nil {
 			ginContext.AbortWithStatus(403)
 			return
 		}
 
 		logrus.Tracef("Handle new YJS client")
-		yjsConnectionHandler(ginContext.Writer, ginContext.Request)
+		documentName := ginContext.Param("documentName")
+		parts, isCanonicalRoom := canonicalYjsRoomParts(documentName)
+		if isCanonicalRoom {
+			roomName, readOnly, status, authorizeErr := authorizeYjsRoom(cruds, user, parts[0], parts[1], parts[2])
+			if status != 0 {
+				if authorizeErr != nil {
+					logrus.Errorf("failed to authorize YJS room: %v", authorizeErr)
+				}
+				ginContext.AbortWithStatus(status)
+				return
+			}
+			serveYjsRoom(ginContext, yjsConnectionHandler, roomName, readOnly)
+			return
+		}
+
+		serveYjsRoom(ginContext, yjsConnectionHandler, documentName, false)
 
 	})
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces robust authorization checks for YJS endpoints. It now verifies user permissions against the underlying database resources (e.g., calendar entries) for canonical YJS room names and file-backed columns, ensuring that only authorized users can access or modify the collaborative documents.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 28 occurrences**

## GHSA-4hrh-34w3-5ww8 · github.com/fabiolb/fabio

2026-11-20 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The Fabio proxy did not normalize incoming request paths before performing routing and access control checks. This allowed an attacker to use specially crafted, unnormalized URLs (e.g., with `../` or percent-encoded characters) to bypass security rules that were based on the expected, normalized path.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass access control restrictions, potentially gaining unauthorized access to internal services or sensitive endpoints that would otherwise be protected by the proxy's routing rules.

### Key diff

```
--- a/proxy/http_proxy.go
+++ b/proxy/http_proxy.go
@@ -93,6 +94,9 @@ func (p *HTTPProxy) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
 		r.Header.Set(p.Config.RequestID, id())
 	}
 
+	// Normalize Paths before routing.
+	r.URL.Path = normalizePath(r.URL.Path)
+
 	t := p.Lookup(r)
 
 	if t == nil {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `normalizePath` function that cleans and normalizes the URL path by handling percent-encoding and resolving `.` and `..` segments. This function is now called on `r.URL.Path` before any routing or lookup operations, ensuring that all path-based security checks are performed on a consistent, canonical path.

## GHSA-jwgx-mp9m-jwcr · @appium/storage-plugin

2026-11-20 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_DELETE`

### Root cause

The `deleteStorageItem` endpoint directly used user-supplied input for the file name without proper validation or sanitization. While `requireValidItemOptions` existed, it was not called for deletion operations, allowing an attacker to craft a malicious filename containing path traversal sequences.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could delete arbitrary files or directories on the server's file system by providing a path traversal sequence (e.g., `../../../../etc/passwd`) as the item name.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/storage-plugin/lib/plugin.ts
+++ b/packages/storage-plugin/lib/plugin.ts
@@ -77,9 +82,17 @@ STORAGE_HANDLERS.listStorageItems = async function listStorageItems(): Promise<S
 STORAGE_HANDLERS.deleteStorageItem = async function deleteStorageItem(
   req: Request,
 ): Promise<boolean> {
-  return await executeStorageMethod(
-    async (storage: Storage) => await storage.delete(parseRequestArgs(req, ['name']).name),
-  );
+  let name: string;
+  try {
+    name = parseRequestArgs(req, ['name']).name;
+    validateStorageItemName(name);
+  } catch (e) {
+    log.error(
+      `Failed to parse the request body for deleting a storage item: ${(e as Error).message}`,
+    );
+    return false;
+  }
+  return await executeStorageMethod(async (storage: Storage) => await storage.delete(name));
 };
 
 STORAGE_HANDLERS.resetStorage = async function resetStorage(): Promise<void> {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `validateStorageItemName` function, which encapsulates the name validation logic. This function is now explicitly called in the `deleteStorageItem` handler to ensure that the provided item name is properly sanitized before being used in file system operations.

## GHSA-66q9-mxf2-xqw6 · nilsteampassnet/TeamPass

2026-11-20 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application's `folders.queries.php` endpoint lacked proper session authentication and granular authorization checks. This allowed unauthenticated users to interact with the endpoint and authenticated users to manipulate folders they did not own or have access to, including renaming, re-parenting, and changing folder types (personal/shared).

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authentication to perform folder operations, or an authenticated user could gain unauthorized access to, modify, or steal other users' or shared folders, leading to data exposure, manipulation, or denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/app/sources/folders.queries.php
+++ b/app/sources/folders.queries.php
@@ -66,6 +66,23 @@
     ]
 );
 
+// Global authorization gate for EVERY action in this file (GHSA-66q9-mxf2-xqw6).
+// Historically folders.queries.php had no session gate: on a cookie-less request
+// $session->get('key') is null and a missing POST 'key' is also null, so the per-case
+// "$post_key !== key" guards compared null !== null and failed open. Require a real
+// authenticated session and enforce DB-driven session validity (admin "kick").
+// Note: userAccessPage('folders') is intentionally NOT used here — it is manager/admin
+// only, while normal users legitimately manage their personal folders through this backend.
+if (
+    (int) ($session->get('user-id') ?? 0) <= 0
+    || empty($session->get('key')) === true
+    || $checkUserAccess->checkSession() === false
+) {
+    $session->set('system-error_code', ERR_NOT_ALLOWED);
+    include TEAMPASS_ROOT . '/public/error.php';
+    exit;
+}
+
 // Define Timezone
 date_default_timezone_set($SETTINGS['timezone'] ?? 'UTC');
 
@@ -400,7 +417,27 @@
                 WHERE id = %i',
                 $inputData['id']
             );
-            
+
+            // Authorization: the caller must have access to the folder being edited.
+            // Without this, any folder id could be renamed/re-parented (IDOR — GHSA-66q9-mxf2-xqw6).
+            if (
+                (int) $session->get('user-admin') !== 1
+                && in_array(
+                    (int) $inputData['id'],
+                    array_map('intval', (array) $session->get('user-accessible_folders')),
+                    true
+                ) === false
+            ) {
+                echo prepareExchangedData(
+                    array(
+                        'error' => true,
+                        'message' => $lang->get('error_not_allowed_to'),
+                    ),
+                    'encode'
+                );
+                break;
+            }
+
             // Check if duplicate folder names are allowed (rename case)
             if (
                 isset($SETTINGS['duplicate_folder']) === true
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a global session authentication gate at the beginning of `folders.queries.php` to ensure all requests are from authenticated and valid sessions. Additionally, specific authorization checks were added for folder editing and re-parenting actions, verifying that the user has access to both the folder being modified and the target parent folder, and preventing cross-domain moves (shared to personal) for non-admin users.

## GHSA-prhx-9q2h-ph2x · paymenter/paymenter

2026-11-20 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The `HasConfirmation` trait, which was used by Livewire components, contained an `executeConfirmedAction` method. This method directly invoked any method name passed to it via the `$action` parameter without proper authentication or authorization checks, allowing unauthenticated users to call arbitrary methods on Livewire components.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could invoke any public method on any Livewire component that used the `HasConfirmation` trait, potentially leading to sensitive data disclosure, unauthorized state changes, or other malicious actions depending on the available methods.

### Key diff

```
--- a/app/Livewire/Traits/HasConfirmation.php
+++ /dev/null
@@ -1,27 +0,0 @@
-<?php
-...
-    public function executeConfirmedAction($action, $parameters = [])
-    {
-        if (method_exists($this, $action)) {
-            return $this->{$action}(...$parameters);
-        }
-...
--- a/app/Livewire/Component.php
+++ b/app/Livewire/Component.php
@@ -9,7 +9,7 @@
 
 class Component extends \Livewire\Component
 {
-    use Disabled, HasConfirmation, HasNotifications;
+    use Disabled, HasNotifications;
 
     public function paginationView()
```

### Fix summary

The `HasConfirmation` trait, which contained the vulnerable `executeConfirmedAction` method, was completely removed. Consequently, its usage was also removed from the base `app/Livewire/Component.php` class, eliminating the arbitrary method invocation vulnerability.

## GHSA-v9v2-98xq-627c · github.com/prest/prest/v2

2026-11-20 · Go · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→SQL`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete fix for a previous SQL injection. Specifically, the `_groupby` function-expression path used a weaker validation function (`isSafeSQLExpression`) compared to `_select`'s `sanitizeSelectField`, allowing malicious input in HTTP headers to be interpolated into SQL queries without proper sanitization.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary SQL commands into database queries by manipulating HTTP headers, potentially leading to unauthorized data access, modification, or deletion, and even remote code execution depending on the database configuration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/controllers/script.go
+++ b/controllers/script.go
@@ -103,10 +103,16 @@ func extractHeaders(rq *http.Request, templateData map[string]interface{}) {
 
 	for key, value := range rq.Header {
 		if len(value) == 1 {
-			headers[key] = value[0]
+			headers[key] = sanitizeScriptParam(value[0])
 			continue
 		}
-		headers[key] = value
+		sanitized := make([]string, 0, len(value))
+		for _, v := range value {
+			sanitized = append(sanitized, sanitizeScriptParam(v))
+		}
+		headers[key] = sanitized
 	}
 
 	templateData["header"] = headers
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the SQL injection by sanitizing HTTP header values before they are used in SQL templates, using the `sanitizeScriptParam` function. It also introduces a new `CacheKey` function that incorporates a SHA-256 hash of the username into the cache key to prevent information leakage between users with different field permissions.

## GHSA-wcqj-wx85-c75p · amqtt

2026-11-20 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from a memory leak during unmanaged QoS 2 session handshakes. This implies that the server failed to properly release memory resources after handling certain types of MQTT QoS 2 packets, especially when sessions were not fully established or managed correctly.

### Impact

An attacker could repeatedly trigger the memory leak by initiating specific QoS 2 handshakes, leading to the server's memory being exhausted. This would eventually cause the aMQTT broker to crash or become unresponsive, resulting in a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- __version__ = "0.12.0"
+++ __version__ = "0.12.1"
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff only shows a version bump, indicating that the actual fix for the memory leak in QoS 2 session handshakes was implemented in the underlying code changes between versions 0.12.0 and 0.12.1, which are not visible in this specific diff. The patch likely involved correctly managing memory allocation and deallocation for QoS 2 packets and sessions.

## GHSA-9657-8vc2-7g2p · poweradmin/poweradmin

2026-11-20 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `WEAK_CRYPTO→RANDOM`

### Root cause

The email second-factor authentication code was generated using `mt_rand()`, which is cryptographically weak and predictable. Additionally, there was no rate limiting or lockout mechanism for verification attempts, allowing an attacker to brute-force the 6-digit code within its validity window if they already possessed the user's password.

### Impact

An attacker who knows a user's password could bypass the email-based two-factor authentication by brute-forcing the 6-digit code, thereby gaining full access to the user's account.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/Domain/Service/MfaService.php
+++ b/lib/Domain/Service/MfaService.php
@@ -199,10 +205,9 @@ public function isValidTotpSecret(?string $secret): bool
      */
     public function generateEmailVerificationCode(): string
     {
-        // Generate a simple numeric verification code (6 digits)
-        $numericCode = mt_rand(100000, 999999);
-        // Convert to string explicitly
-        return (string)$numericCode;
+        // A second factor must not come from a predictable generator: mt_rand()
+        // state is recoverable from codes an attacker mints on their own account.
+        return (string)random_int(100000, 999999);
     }
 
     /**
@@ -261,6 +266,73 @@ public function disableMfa(int $userId): ?UserMfa
      * @return bool True if the code is valid, false otherwise
      */
     public function verifyCode(int $userId, string $code): bool
+    {
+        if ($this->isVerificationLocked($userId)) {
+            $this->logger->warning('Verification refused - too many failed attempts for user ID: {userId}', ['userId' => $userId]);
+            return false;
+        }
+
+        $isValid = $this->checkCode($userId, $code);
+        $this->recordVerificationOutcome($userId, $isValid);
+
+        return $isValid;
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Whether verification is currently refused for this user because the
+     * failed-attempt ceiling was reached.
+     */
+    private function isVerificationLocked(int $userId): bool
+    {
+        $userMfa = $this->userMfaRepository->findByUserId($userId);
+
+        if (!$userMfa) {
+            return false;
+        }
+
+        return (int)($userMfa->getVerificationDataAsArray()['locked_until'] ?? 0) > time();
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Count the attempt and start refusing once the ceiling is reached.
+     *
+     * Re-reads the record because checkCode() saves its own changes (recovery
+     * code consumed, code marked used); writing a stale copy would undo them.
+     */
+    private function recordVerificationOutcome(int $userId, bool $isValid): void
+    {
+        $userMfa = $this->userMfaRepository->findByUserId($userId);
+
+        if (!$userMfa) {
+            return;
+        }
+
+        $metadata = $userMfa->getVerificationDataAsArray();
+
+        if ($isValid) {
+            // Nothing accrued, so skip the write a clean verification would add.
+            if (empty($metadata['failed_attempts']) && empty($metadata['locked_until'])) {
+                return;
+            }
+
+            unset($metadata['failed_attempts'], $metadata['locked_until']);
+        } else {
+            $failed = (int)($metadata['failed_attempts'] ?? 0) + 1;
+
+            if ($failed >= self::MAX_VERIFY_ATTEMPTS) {
+                $metadata['failed_attempts'] = 0;
+                $metadata['locked_until'] = time() + self::VERIFY_LOCKOUT_SECONDS;
+                $this->logger->warning('Second factor locked after repeated failures for user ID: {userId}', ['userId' => $userId]);
+            } else {
+                $metadata['failed_attempts'] = $failed;
+            }
+        }
+
+        $userMfa->setVerificationData($metadata);
+        $this->userMfaRepository->save($userMfa);
+    }
+
+    private function checkCode(int $userId, string $code): bool
     {
         $userMfa = $this->userMfaRepository->findByUserId($userId);
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces `mt_rand()` with `random_int()` for generating the email verification code, making it cryptographically secure. It also introduces a rate-limiting mechanism, locking out verification attempts after 5 failures for 15 minutes (900 seconds), and ensures that lockout state persists across code regenerations.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 8 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, Erlang, JavaScript, Go, Python
> → GHSA-cj8j-37rh-8475
> → GHSA-2528-jw5q-ww88
> → GHSA-v9gv-xp36-jgj8
> → GHSA-vh45-f885-3848
> → GHSA-rg76-677x-56q9
> → GHSA-w67g-5rqw-f597
> → GHSA-jxw3-mjmx-3pqm

## GHSA-6499-jgj4-2wpf · ckan

2026-11-20 · Python · **MODERATE** 6.8 · Pattern: `SESSION_FIXATION→HIJACK`

### Root cause

The application did not regenerate the user's session ID after a successful login or registration. This allowed an attacker to provide a user with a pre-determined session ID, which would remain valid after the user authenticated.

### Impact

An attacker could fixate a user's session ID, and if they could obtain this ID (e.g., via XSS or by simply knowing the pre-set ID), they could hijack the user's authenticated session after the user logs in.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ckan/views/user.py
+++ b/ckan/views/user.py
@@ -472,6 +472,7 @@ def post(self) -> Union[Response, str]:
         # log the user in programatically
         userobj = model.User.get(user_dict["id"])
         if userobj:
+            regenerate_session()
             login_user(userobj)
             rotate_token()
         resp = h.redirect_to(u'user.me')
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new helper function `regenerate_session()` and calls it after successful user registration and login. This ensures that a new session ID is generated, invalidating any previously fixed session ID.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 7 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, Python, JavaScript, Go
> → GHSA-gj7p-595x-qwf5
> → GHSA-hg7g-56h5-5pqr
> → GHSA-fj52-5g4h-gmq8
> → GHSA-94jp-7776-qj6q
> → GHSA-jq7h-wrvp-3rgx
> → GHSA-6j3h-rp73-6rvf

## GHSA-fjjq-8hw5-cf9f · uu_split

2026-11-20 · RUST · **MODERATE** 6.3 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The `split` utility on Windows initially opened output files using `O_TRUNC` based on a path. This created a race condition where, after the file path was checked but before the file was actually truncated, an attacker could swap the intended output file with a symbolic link pointing to an arbitrary file (e.g., the input file or another sensitive file). The subsequent `O_TRUNC` operation would then truncate the attacker-controlled target.

### Impact

An attacker could cause the `split` utility to truncate arbitrary files on the system, including its own input file, leading to data loss or denial of service for other applications relying on those files.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/uu/split/src/platform/windows.rs
+++ b/src/uu/split/src/platform/windows.rs
@@ -19,16 +19,7 @@ pub fn instantiate_current_writer(
     is_new: bool,
 ) -> Result<BufWriter<Box<dyn Write>>> {
     let file = if is_new {
-        // create new file
-        std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
-            .write(true)
-            .create(true)
-            .truncate(true)
-            .open(Path::new(filename))
-            .map_err(|e| match e.kind() {
-                ErrorKind::IsADirectory => Error::other(
-                    translate!("split-error-is-a-directory", "dir" => filename.quote()),
-                ),
-                _ => Error::other(
-                    translate!("split-error-unable-to-open-file", "file" => filename.quote()),
-                ),
-            })?
+        create_or_truncate_output_file(input, filename)?
     } else {
         // re-open file that we previously created to append to it
         let file = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the file opening logic to first attempt creating new files atomically using `O_CREAT|O_EXCL`. If the file already exists, it is opened without `O_TRUNC`, and an explicit `ftruncate` is performed only after an additional check ensures that the output file does not refer to the input file, preventing self-truncation via symlinks.

## GHSA-vcx7-pfrh-mcfg · storyblok-backup

2026-11-20 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.7 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The application was creating backup files using Storyblok component names directly in the file path without proper sanitization. This allowed special characters like '/', '\', and '..' to be included in the filename, which could manipulate the intended file storage location.

### Impact

An attacker could craft component names to write backup files to arbitrary locations on the file system, potentially overwriting existing files or creating new ones outside the intended backup directory.

### Key diff

```
--- a/bin/storyblok-backup.mjs
+++ b/bin/storyblok-backup.mjs
@@ -238,6 +238,12 @@ const writeJson = (folder, file, content) => {
 		if (folder !== null) {
 			outputFile += `/${folder}`
 		}
+
+		// Sanitize file name
+		file = file.replaceAll('/', '_')
+		file = file.replaceAll('\\', '_')
+		file = file.replaceAll('..', '_')
+
 		outputFile += `/${file}.json`
 		fs.writeFileSync(outputFile, JSON.stringify(content, null, 2), (error) => {
 			if (error) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces sanitization for the 'file' variable before it's used to construct the output file path. It replaces all occurrences of '/', '\', and '..' with underscores, preventing directory traversal characters from being part of the filename.

## GHSA-3g82-77xr-68x5 · js-toml

2026-11-20 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `js-toml` library's `load()` function, when processing deeply nested TOML input, used uncontrolled recursion in its parsing and interpretation logic. This allowed an attacker to provide a specially crafted input that would cause the call stack to exhaust, leading to a `RangeError` (stack overflow).

### Impact

An attacker could supply a malicious TOML string to an application using `js-toml`, causing the application to crash due to a stack exhaustion error, effectively leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/load/load.ts
+++ b/src/load/load.ts
@@ -21,11 +21,20 @@ export const load = (toml: string): Record<string, unknown> => {
   if (lexingResult.errors.length > 0) {
     throw new LexerError(lexingResult.errors);
   }
-
-  parser.input = lexingResult.tokens;
-  const cst = parser.toml();
-
-  if (parser.errors.length > 0) {
-    throw new ParserError(parser.errors);
-  }
+
+  checkNestingDepth(lexingResult.tokens, maxDepth);
+
+  try {
+    parser.input = lexingResult.tokens;
+    const cst = parser.toml();
+
+    if (parser.errors.length > 0) {
+      throw new ParserError(parser.errors);
+    }
+
+    interpreter.maxDepth = maxDepth;
+    return interpreter.visit(cst);
+  } catch (error) {
+    if (error instanceof RangeError) {
+      throw new SyntaxParseError(
+        'Maximum nesting depth exceeded (stack overflow)'
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `maxDepth` limit (defaulting to 100) to both the lexer and interpreter. It adds a `checkNestingDepth` function to pre-scan tokens for excessive array/inline-table nesting and `checkKeyDepth` in the interpreter to limit dotted-key/table-header depth. A `DepthLimitError` is thrown if the limit is exceeded, and `RangeError`s are caught and re-thrown as `SyntaxParseError` for consistency.

## GHSA-cg26-4c6j-2x8h · io.netty.incubator:netty-incubator-codec-ohttp

2026-11-20 · Java · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The OHTTP codecs (both client and server) were not properly releasing or discarding read bytes from their internal cumulation buffers after an OHTTP exchange was completed. This meant that memory used by processed HTTP content was retained, leading to a gradual increase in memory usage.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially cause a denial of service by continuously sending OHTTP requests, leading to memory exhaustion and eventual application crash or instability.

### Key diff

```
-                            destroyContext();
+                            if (cumulationBuffer.isReadable()) {
+                                cumulationBuffer.discardSomeReadBytes();
+                            } else {
+                                cumulationBuffer.release();
+                                cumulationBuffer = Unpooled.EMPTY_BUFFER;
+                            }
+                            destroyContext();
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds logic to explicitly discard read bytes or release the entire cumulation buffer and set it to an empty buffer once an OHTTP exchange is marked as complete (i.e., when the last HTTP content is received). This ensures that memory is reclaimed promptly.

## GHSA-9rv8-9hvg-92mq · uu_chcon

2026-11-20 · RUST · **MODERATE** 4.5 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The original code used `fts_read` to traverse directories and obtain file metadata. However, when processing files recursively, it did not anchor the operations to a directory file descriptor. This meant that between the time `fts_read` checked a file's metadata and when `chcon` attempted to modify its security context, a malicious actor could replace or modify the file, leading to a TOCTOU vulnerability.

### Impact

An attacker could exploit this TOCTOU vulnerability to manipulate which file's security context is changed, potentially leading to privilege escalation or unauthorized access by applying an incorrect security context to a sensitive file.

### Key diff

```
-    let mut entry = fts.last_entry_ref().unwrap();
+    let (file_full_name, fts_access_path, mut result) = {
+        let mut entry = fts
+            .last_entry_ref()
+            .expect("last_entry_ref is present after successful read_next_entry");
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the use of `rustix::fs::openat` with `O_RDONLY | O_CLOEXEC` flags to open files relative to a directory file descriptor. This ensures that subsequent operations, such as changing the security context, are performed on the exact file that was opened, preventing race conditions where the file could be swapped out from under the program.

## GHSA-xhrc-7f76-vh96 · uu_env

2026-11-20 · RUST · **LOW** 3.3 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `uu_env` utility's `split_iterator` incorrectly handled backslashes within single-quoted strings. Instead of treating them literally as GNU `env` does, it either attempted to interpret them as escape sequences or rejected them as invalid, leading to a discrepancy in how commands were parsed compared to the expected behavior.

### Impact

An attacker could craft environment variable assignments that, when processed by `uu_env`, would be parsed differently than intended by GNU `env`. This could potentially lead to unexpected command execution or manipulation of environment variables in a way not foreseen by the user or calling application.

### Key diff

```
-            Some(c) if REPLACEMENTS.iter().any(|&x| x.0 == c) => {
-                self.push_char_to_word(BACKSLASH);
-                Ok(())
-            }
-            Some(c) => Err(self.make_invalid_sequence_backslash_xin_minus_s(c)),
+            Some(_) => {
+                self.push_char_to_word(BACKSLASH);
+                Ok(())
+            }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `split_iterator` to correctly handle backslashes within single-quoted strings. It removes the logic that tried to interpret or reject backslashes, ensuring that they are always treated as literal characters, aligning its behavior with GNU `env`.

## GHSA-2hjf-7455-w946 · amqtt

2026-11-20 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The application used regular expressions to validate and match MQTT topic filters. Specifically, it dynamically constructed regex patterns from user-supplied topic filters, which could lead to ReDoS (Regular Expression Denial of Service) if a malicious or complex filter was provided. Additionally, the initial validation logic for MQTT topic filter wildcards ('#' and '+') was incomplete, allowing malformed filters to bypass checks and potentially cause issues with the regex engine.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a specially designed MQTT topic filter that, when processed by the broker's regular expression engine, would consume excessive CPU resources, leading to a Denial of Service (DoS) for the broker. This could make the broker unresponsive to legitimate clients.

### Key diff

```
--- a/amqtt/broker.py
+++ b/amqtt/broker.py
@@ -1123,14 +1124,20 @@ def _matches(self, topic: str, a_filter: str) -> bool:
             # if filter doesn't contain wildcard, return exact match
             return a_filter == topic
 
-        # else use regex (re.compile is an expensive operation, store the matcher for future use)
-        if a_filter not in self._topic_filter_matchers:
-            self._topic_filter_matchers[a_filter] = re.compile(re.escape(a_filter)
-                                                               .replace("\#", "?.*")
-                                                               .replace("\+", "[^/]*")
-                                                               .lstrip("?"))
-        match_pattern = self._topic_filter_matchers[a_filter]
-        return bool(match_pattern.fullmatch(topic))
+        sub_levels = a_filter.split("/")
+        pub_levels = topic.split("/")
+
+        for i, level in enumerate(sub_levels):
+            if ("+" in level and level != "+") or ("#" in level and level != "#"):
+                return False
+
+            if level == "#":
+                return i == len(sub_levels) - 1
+
+            if i >= len(pub_levels) or level not in ("+", pub_levels[i]):
+                return False
+
+        return len(sub_levels) == len(pub_levels)
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the use of regular expressions for topic filter matching. Instead, it implements a manual, level-by-level parsing and comparison logic for MQTT topic filters and topics. It also strengthens the input validation for topic filters, ensuring that wildcard characters ('#' and '+') are used correctly according to MQTT specifications before any matching occurs.

## GHSA-3h6w-xcw3-9xjj · io.openremote:openremote-manager

2026-11-20 · Java · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the OpenRemote manager not properly enforcing realm activity checks for certain user management operations. This allowed an attacker to perform actions like disconnecting MQTT sessions for users in inactive realms without proper authorization, bypassing the intended security controls.

### Impact

An attacker could disconnect MQTT sessions for users in inactive realms, potentially disrupting services or user connectivity, even if they were not authorized to manage users in that specific realm.

### Key diff

```
--- a/model/src/main/java/org/openremote/model/security/UserResource.java
+++ b/model/src/main/java/org/openremote/model/security/UserResource.java
@@ -44,9 +44,20 @@
 @Tag(
     name = "User",
     description =
-        "Query and manage identity-provider users, roles, credentials, locale, and live sessions")
+        "Query and manage identity-provider users, roles, credentials, locale, and live sessions. "
+            + "An active realm is enabled and its activation time has been reached. "
+            + "Superusers bypass realm activity and cross-realm restrictions; endpoint-specific role requirements still apply.")
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds explicit checks to ensure that the target realm for user management operations must be active, unless the caller is a superuser. This prevents unauthorized actions on inactive realms.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 148 occurrences**

## GHSA-9x8x-crjq-g64h · jupyter-mcp-server

2026-11-20 · Python · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The Jupyter MCP Server exposed management routes (e.g., /api/connect, /api/stop) that were intended to be accessible only from localhost. However, it lacked proper validation of the Host and Origin headers, making it vulnerable to DNS rebinding attacks. An attacker could trick a browser into making requests to these routes, bypassing the localhost restriction.

### Impact

An attacker could hijack the Jupyter backend by forcing a user's browser to connect to an attacker-controlled document or stop the Jupyter MCP Server, leading to denial of service or unauthorized control over the Jupyter environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/jupyter_mcp_server/server.py
+++ b/jupyter_mcp_server/server.py
@@ -77,6 +79,74 @@ async def verify_token(self, token: str) -> AccessToken | None:
         return AccessToken(token=token, client_id="mcp-client", scopes=[])
 
 
+MANAGEMENT_ROUTE_PATHS = {"/api/connect", "/api/stop", "/api/healthz"}
+AUTHENTICATED_MANAGEMENT_ROUTE_PATHS = {"/api/connect", "/api/stop"}
+LOCAL_HOSTNAMES = {"localhost", "127.0.0.1", "::1"}
+
+class ManagementRouteSecurityMiddleware(BaseHTTPMiddleware):
+    async def dispatch(self, request: Request, call_next):
+        if not _is_management_route(request.url.path):
+            return await call_next(request)
+
+        if not _is_local_hostname(request.url.hostname):
+            return JSONResponse({"error": "Invalid Host header"}, status_code=421)
+
+        origin = request.headers.get("origin")
+        if origin:
+            origin_hostname = urlsplit(origin).hostname
+            if not _is_local_hostname(origin_hostname):
+                return JSONResponse({"error": "Invalid Origin header"}, status_code=403)
+
+        if self._token_verifier and _is_authenticated_management_route(request.url.path):
+            # ... authentication logic ...
+
+        return await call_next(request)
```

### Fix summary

A new `ManagementRouteSecurityMiddleware` was introduced. This middleware explicitly checks the `Host` header to ensure it's a local hostname (localhost, 127.0.0.1, ::1) and, if an `Origin` header is present, validates that its hostname is also local. Additionally, it enforces bearer token authentication for sensitive management routes like `/api/connect` and `/api/stop`.

## GHSA-p32v-8v8j-j534 · Refitter

2026-11-20 · C# · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The Refitter library was generating HTTP client code that included 'Accept' and 'Content-Type' header values directly from OpenAPI specifications without proper escaping. If a malicious OpenAPI specification contained specially crafted content-type values, these could be injected into the generated C# code, leading to arbitrary code execution when the generated code was compiled and run.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious OpenAPI specification that, when used with Refitter, would generate code capable of executing arbitrary commands on the developer's machine during compilation or runtime of the generated client.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Refitter.Core/Generation/MethodAttributeGenerator.cs
+++ b/src/Refitter.Core/Generation/MethodAttributeGenerator.cs
-                headers.Add($"\"Accept: {string.Join(", ", uniqueContentTypes)}\"");
+                headers.Add($"\"Accept: {string.Join(", ", uniqueContentTypes.Select(ParameterShared.EscapeString))}\"");
-                headers.Add($"\"Content-Type: {contentType}\"");
+                headers.Add($"\"Content-Type: {ParameterShared.EscapeString(contentType)}\"");
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `ParameterShared.EscapeString` utility function. This function is now applied to all content-type values before they are incorporated into the generated 'Accept' and 'Content-Type' HTTP header strings, ensuring that any special characters are properly escaped and cannot be interpreted as code.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 143 occurrences**

## GHSA-w3f2-pr6x-wwjc · github.com/git-lfs/git-lfs/v3

2026-11-20 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because Git LFS was constructing SSH commands by directly concatenating user-controlled input (specifically, the 'meta.Path' which could contain repository names) into the command string without proper escaping. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary commands by crafting a malicious repository name containing shell metacharacters.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the client machine when a user interacts with a specially crafted Git LFS repository URL. This could lead to remote code execution.

### Key diff

```
Before: `args = append(args, fmt.Sprintf("%s %s %s", command, meta.Path, operation))`
After: `args = append(args, strings.Join(subprocess.ShellQuote([]string{command, meta.Path, operation}), " "))`
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by properly quoting the user-controlled input (meta.Path) before it is included in the SSH command string. It uses `subprocess.ShellQuote` to ensure that special characters in the repository path are escaped, preventing command injection.

## GHSA-x72p-hmqp-46hw · github.com/git-lfs/git-lfs/v3

2026-11-20 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

Git LFS was overly permissive in parsing '.lfsconfig' files, allowing non-LFS related 'access' keys to be processed. An attacker could craft a malicious '.lfsconfig' file with an 'access' key that, when processed by Git LFS, would lead to the execution of arbitrary commands.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary command execution on a user's system by tricking them into cloning a repository with a specially crafted '.lfsconfig' file.

### Key diff

```
--- a/config/git_fetcher.go
+++ b/config/git_fetcher.go
@@ -88,7 +88,7 @@ func readGitConfig(configs ...*git.ConfigurationSource) (gf *GitFetcher, extensi
 				allowed = true
 				remote := strings.Join(parts[1:len(parts)-1], ".")
 				uniqRemotes[remote] = remote == "origin"
-			} else if len(parts) > 2 && parts[len(parts)-1] == "access" {
+			} else if len(parts) > 2 && parts[0] == "lfs" && parts[len(parts)-1] == "access" {
 				allowed = true
```

### Fix summary

The patch restricts the parsing of 'access' keys in '.lfsconfig' files to only those explicitly prefixed with 'lfs'. This ensures that only legitimate Git LFS configuration keys are processed, preventing the abuse of other 'access' keys for command execution.

## GHSA-x7xj-4j4f-88xr · github.com/git-lfs/git-lfs/v3

2026-11-20 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

Git LFS's HTTP client did not properly restrict the forwarding of sensitive HTTP headers, specifically 'Cookie' headers, across cross-origin redirects. While 'Authorization' headers were already handled, 'Cookie' headers were not, allowing them to be inadvertently sent to untrusted third-party servers after a redirection.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially craft a malicious Git LFS server that redirects a client to a different origin, causing the client to leak sensitive 'Cookie' information (e.g., session tokens) to the attacker's controlled domain.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lfshttp/client.go
+++ b/lfshttp/client.go
@@ -594,7 +594,7 @@ func newRequestForRetry(req *http.Request, location string) (*http.Request, erro
 	sameOrigin := req.URL.Scheme == newReq.URL.Scheme && req.URL.Host == newReq.URL.Host
 	for key := range req.Header {
 		// Credentials must not follow cross-origin redirects, even HTTP-to-HTTPS upgrades.
-		if !sameOrigin && key == "Authorization" {
+		if !sameOrigin && (key == "Authorization" || key == "Cookie") {
 			continue
 		}
 		newReq.Header.Set(key, req.Header.Get(key))
```

### Fix summary

The patch extends the logic for preventing sensitive header forwarding to include 'Cookie' headers when a cross-origin redirect occurs. It modifies the `newRequestForRetry` function to explicitly check for and exclude 'Cookie' headers in addition to 'Authorization' headers if the redirect is cross-origin. Test cases were also updated to verify this new behavior.
